#### Abstracts
##### **ECG for laboratory values:A comprehensive physiological and technical analysis**
2610.28507v1 by Walentin Palanchuk

Laboratory measurements are essential for clinical decision making but typically require invasive blood sampling. Recent research suggests that ECG recordings may contain information beyond cardiac rhythm and provide information related to laboratory values. This thesis investigates NT-proBNP prediction from ECG recordings and patient metadata using a multimodal deep learning framework, while considering the physiological plausibility of ECG-based prediction. It also compares a 1-lead configuration using lead II with the corresponding 12-lead configuration. Additional experiments on glucose, hemoglobin, and potassium are presented in the appendix.
  Regression, binary classification, and three-class classification experiments were conducted using MIMIC-IV, with the three-class task based on clinically established thresholds. The 12-lead configuration consistently achieved better performance. Regression reached a mean absolute error of 534.98 pg/mL for 1-lead and 514.27 pg/mL for 12-lead. Binary classification achieved AUROCs of 0.849 and 0.876, respectively, while three-class classification achieved macro-averaged AUROCs of 0.738 and 0.752.
  The results indicate that precise NT-proBNP regression remains challenging, whereas broader concentration ranges can be classified more effectively. The findings are consistent with the physiological hypothesis that ECG characteristics and NT-proBNP concentrations reflect shared cardiovascular processes. ECG-based NT-proBNP prediction may therefore provide complementary information, particularly for classifying broader concentration ranges. However, laboratory measurements remain necessary, and validation on independent datasets is required.

摘要：

##### **Brain2Vec: A Self-Supervised 3D Foundation Model for Structural Brain MRI**
2610.28411v1 by Spiros Maggioros, Guray Erus, Gareth Harman, George Aidinis, Alexander Getka, Aristeidis Sotiras, Christos Davatzikos

Deep learning models have become essential tools in medical imaging, yet training them end-to-end for diverse tasks remains challenging due to limited labeled data and computational constraints. To address this, we introduce Brain2Vec, a foundation model pre-trained on 74,425 T1-weighted Magnetic Resonance Imaging (MRI) scans pooled from multiple studies. Brain2Vec can serve both as a weight initialization backbone for fine-tuning downstream tasks and as a standalone feature extractor. We evaluated Brain2Vec in three tasks: downstream predictive performance, convergence speed, and embedding quality. Using Brain2Vec's pretrained weights as initialization and fine-tuning on labeled data for a brain age prediction task, results demonstrate improved final accuracy and faster convergence compared to random initialization. Additionally, its frozen embeddings outperform anatomically defined volumetric features on four of five downstream classification tasks and perform competitively with a state-of-the-art foundation model pre-trained on substantially more data. Lastly, we show that Brain2Vec encodes information primarily from a spatially localized and neuroanatomically meaningful set of brain regions, and that manipulating a subset of latent features produces monotonic, region-specific effects on the reconstructed anatomy. Brain2Vec code and trained models are available as an open-source package on https://github.com/CBICA/Brain2Vec. Users can apply Brain2Vec locally by installing the package, or directly through the NiChart cloud platform https://cloud.neuroimagingchart.com, where Brain2Vec features can be derived without requiring local installation or specialized infrastructure.

摘要：

##### **Gated Prototype Transfer for Zero-Support Medical Vision Language Model**
2610.28350v1 by Sumaiya Fazal, Rizwan Qureshi, Laleh Behjat, Samira Ebrahimi Kahou

Realistic few-shot adaptation of medical vision-language models (VLMs) can leave low-prevalence classes without any labeled support. The training-free SS- Text+ solver remains operational in this setting, but its zero-support prototypes receive no visual adaptation and fall back to a purely textual class prototype. We in- troduce Gated Knowledge-Guided Prototype Transfer (GProtoT), a pre-repulsion module that transfers supported-class visual residuals to missing classes using VLM similarity and an ontology-based clinical distance. A validation-free evidence gate scales transfer based on semantic relevance and residual agreement, thereby limiting unsupported extrapolation. In 50 paired realistic one-shot trials on MESSIDOR with FLAIR features, severe and proliferative diabetic retinopathy were absent from every support set. Relative to SS-Text+, gated ProtoT improved missing-class recall by 5.23 points, average class accuracy by 1.37 points, and the missing/supported recall harmonic mean by 1.83 points. It reduced negative- transfer frequency from 46-48% for ungated transfer to 2%, while at the cost of reductions of 1.20 percentage points in supported-class recall and 0.45 percentage points in top-1 accuracy. These results indicate that evidence-based gating provides a safety mechanism for the knowledge source. GProtoT is based on SS- Text+ and requires no gradients, validation data, or additional model inference, of- fering a low-compute path toward accountable adaptation under incomplete clini- cal supervision. Code is available: https://github.com/sumaiyaafridi/GProtoT

摘要：

##### **VGL-stGen: Learning a shared multimodal model for lung histology and spatial transcriptomics**
2610.28266v1 by Haenara Shin, Dongjoo Lee, Sungwoo Bae, Kwon Joong Na, Daeseung Lee, Hongyoon Choi

VGL-stGen is a multimodal large language model adapted from a pretrained medical vision-language model using paired lung H&E and spatial transcriptomics with single-cell references. Training integrates image-expression and expression-cell-type pairs, and gene-conditioned pseudo-labels transfer transcriptomic cell-type supervision to histology images. One deployed checkpoint supports thirteen evaluated tasks spanning tissue and cell-type classification, gene prediction and conditional expression completion from combinations of histology, cell identity and observed gene expression. Task-specific decoding produces categorical labels, gene lists and structured expression levels. For conditional completion, fixing queried gene identities and predicting their levels autoregressively improves structured prediction. Controlled adaptation shows that restricting supervision to requested expression-level tokens can further improve image-conditioned completion. Image-permutation experiments indicate that correctly paired histology adds information beyond observed expression. External classification relies partly on computational references with shared annotation lineage, and some H&E sources were exposed during cancer training or model development. Within these conditions, VGL-stGen demonstrates how a medical vision-language backbone can learn from spatial transcriptomics and histology to connect tissue morphology and molecular states through shared generative modeling.

摘要：

##### **Africa-Papers: A 10-Billion-Token Multi-Domain Academic Corpus for African Foundation Language Models**
2610.28173v1 by Mich-Seth Owusu

Large Language Models (LLMs) depend fundamentally on the diversity, depth, and factual rigor of their pre-training corpora. However, contemporary foundational pre-training datasets (such as The Pile, S2ORC, and Dolma) exhibit a severe structural omission: African scientific, medical, legal, and agricultural scholarship constitutes less than 0.5% of global pre-training tokens. Consequently, state-of-the-art models suffer from systematic failure modes when reasoning about tropical epidemiology, localized smallholder agro-ecologies, regional economic governance, and indigenous linguistic structures. To resolve this representation deficit and advance African intellectual sovereignty in artificial intelligence, we introduce Africa-Papers, the largest curated multi-domain Pan-African academic pre-training corpus to date. Comprising over 844,807 full-text papers spanning 10.15 billion tokens across 879 Parquet shards, Africa-Papers unifies four primary scientific streams: (1) continental peer-reviewed journals indexed in DOAJ across 31 African nations, (2) the CGIAR CGSpace agricultural repository, (3) African-authored and African-focused STEM/AI preprints on arXiv, and (4) open-access international publications by scholars affiliated with African institutions indexed via OpenAlex. The entire corpus is processed into layout-aware, clean Markdown preserving section hierarchies, mathematical equations, and tabular data, accompanied by exact BPE token counts and a standardized 8-domain taxonomy. We release Africa-Papers openly on the Hugging Face Hub under the AfriSpeech organization to accelerate continual pre-training and domain evaluation for foundation models globally.

摘要：

##### **IECG: Weakly-Supervised Interpretability-Enhancing Foundation Model for Electrocardiogram**
2610.28083v1 by Huynh Dang Nguyen, Trong-Thang Pham, Anh-Thuan Do, Phuong-Nhi Dinh, Brijesh Patel, Ngan Le, Van Nguyen

Electrocardiography (ECG) is a cornerstone of cardiac diagnosis, yet most machine learning methods for ECG analysis lack interpretability, limiting their clinical adoption. We present IECG, a weakly-supervised ECG foundation model that jointly performs diagnostic classification and abnormality localization without requiring manual abnormal region annotations, overcoming a long-standing bottleneck in interpretable ECG learning. Central to IECG is a weakly-supervised attention strategy that explicitly incorporates clinical priors by deriving abnormality heatmap pseudo-labels from established ECG clinical knowledge. These pseudo-labels guide the model's CLS-token attention through a KL-divergence loss, encouraging it to focus on diagnostically relevant waveform regions. During pretraining, IECG is jointly optimized with clinically guided attention supervision, ECG-text contrastive alignment, and report captioning objectives, enabling it to learn rich multimodal representations while producing localized waveform evidence. By explicitly aggregating features from clinically guided attention regions, IECG grounds its diagnostic predictions in interpretable waveform evidence without sacrificing classification performance. Extensive experiments demonstrate that IECG achieves state-of-the-art abnormal region localization while maintaining strong classification performance, advancing trustworthy foundation models for ECG analysis.

摘要：

##### **PULSE: Personalized Opportunistic Learning for Wearable Devices**
2610.28081v1 by Mattia Giovanni Campana, Dariush Salami, Hüseyin Yiğitler, Stephan Sigg

Wearable devices increasingly support personalized health and well-being applications, but post-deployment data can differ substantially from those used for training, while purely local adaptation may be limited by the data available on a single device during real-world operation. Conventional federated and decentralized learning rely on recurrent communication and stable connectivity, whereas existing opportunistic approaches often exploit available encounters without adequately considering their utility, compatibility, or communication cost.
  We propose PULSE, an event-driven framework for personalized opportunistic learning that treats each encounter as an online collaboration decision, selecting either no interaction or a promising nearby peer. Peer gradients are incorporated only when compatible with the local learning direction, while past interactions guide future peer selection. Across human activity recognition, fall detection, and stress recognition under synthetic and real contact traces, PULSE preserves or improves personalized classification performance, while reducing peer-assisted exchanges by 80-94% compared with opportunistic model averaging. On-device experiments further demonstrate its feasibility on wearable hardware: peer-assisted operations require limited energy, while the complete implementation occupies 149.8 KiB of RAM and 561.8 KiB of flash. The obtained results show that selective opportunistic collaboration can provide effective personalization while limiting communication and resource demands on constrained wearable devices.

摘要：

##### **Quantifying the Threat: A Bayesian Probabilistic Framework for Heavy Metal Health Risks among Children in the Densu Basin, Ghana**
2610.28064v1 by T. Ansah-Narh, G. Y. Afrifa, J. B. Tandoh, E. N. N. Nortey, D. Adjei, R. Opoku-Sarkordie, R. Davis

Groundwater in Ghana's Densu River Basin remains a key domestic water source, but multi-metal contamination threatens children's health. Previous deterministic hazard index assessments provide limited treatment of uncertainty, co-exposure, and spatial variability. This study applies a Bayesian probabilistic framework for non-carcinogenic risk assessment using 94 groundwater samples and measurements of Fe, Mn, Ni, Pb, Cd, and As. A Bayesian regression model propagates parameter uncertainty and accounts for contaminant co-occurrence. Adaptive Hamiltonian Monte Carlo is used for posterior inference, while inverse distance weighting generates spatially continuous risk surfaces. Pb, As, and Cd dominate cumulative risk. Posterior predictive results indicate that approximately 31% of sampling sites have a probability greater than 0.5 of exceeding a hazard index (HI) of 1. The risk surface is highly heterogeneous, with a posterior mean of 0.372 and a standard deviation of 0.416. High-risk zones often reflect moderate co-exposure rather than extreme contamination by a single metal. The framework provides uncertainty-aware, spatially explicit information to support groundwater monitoring, risk mitigation, and public health planning in data-limited regions.

摘要：

##### **When Do Humans and Machines Truly Complement Each Other? Formal Conditions and Empirical Evidence for Human-AI Synergy**
2610.27965v1 by Andrea Campagner, Federico Cabitza, Vincenzo Crupi

Can humans and artificial intelligence cooperate such that their combination yields greater accuracy than either agent alone? This result, termed \emph{synergy} or \emph{complementarity}, is notoriously difficult to achieve, even when both agents possess significant independent accuracy. In this paper, we present a formal framework to model human-AI interaction through the lens of reliance patterns, which characterize the joint correctness structure of human and algorithmic decisions. We derive mathematically necessary and sufficient conditions for synergy, demonstrating that its emergence depends on the ability of either agent to identify and correct the errors of the other, what we call Human and Algorithmic Correction. Beyond its theoretical contribution, the proposed framework provides a foundation for the design and evaluation of knowledge-based decision support systems, where interaction policies must dynamically integrate heterogeneous sources of expertise. We demonstrate this through the analysis of seven clinical case studies. Our empirical results reveal a ``dominance trap,'' where a strong algorithmic advantage can paradoxically inhibit synergy by eroding human corrective intervention. We also show that synergy can persist even in the presence of cognitive biases, such as automation bias, provided there is functional de-correlation between human and AI errors. These results provide a formal foundation for key concepts in the human-AI systems literature and offer actionable insights for designing interaction-centric AI systems that foster complementarity.

摘要：

##### **HypTI: Hyperbolic Geometry-Aware Multimodal Feature Fusion Framework for Dementia Detection Based on Tabular-Image Data**
2610.27941v1 by Jianfei Xu, Huizhi Liang, Judith Harrison, John-Paul Taylor, Jaume Bacardit, Varun Ojha

AI-assisted dementia detection usually requires multimodal fusion of diverse data, such as clinical tabular assessments and neuroimaging data. However, most existing approaches perform feature fusion in Euclidean space. This overlooks the geometric structure of representation, including hierarchical organization in clinical assessments and multi-scale anatomical patterns in neuroimaging data. Recent studies have proved hyperbolic space provides a natural geometry for modelling hierarchical and multi-scale structures due to its exponentially expanding space. Motivated by these, we propose HypTI, a hyperbolic geometry-aware multimodal fusion framework for dementia detection based on clinical tabular assessments and MRI scans. HypTI extends hyperbolic representation learning to an underexplored tabular-MRI setting, jointly modelling clinical tabular, MRI scans, and cross-modal interactions in hyperbolic space. Specifically, clinical tabular variables are represented through learnable Lorentz prototypes to capture hierarchical relationships among assessments, while MRI scans are encoded using ROI-guided multi-scale representation learning to capture anatomical patterns across brain regions and spatial scales in Lorentz space. The multimodal representations are integrated through an adaptive fusion module generates subject-specific scale and shift coefficients to dynamically modulate and integrate the multimodal embeddings, enabling effective patient-level tabular-image representation learning for dementia detection. Experiments on OASIS-3 dataset show that the proposed framework achieves strong performance compared with representative approaches.

摘要：

##### **Temporal Evidence Allocation For Longitudinal Clinical Retrieval: A Position And Technical Report**
2610.27682v1 by Soumya Ranjan Sahoo, Divya Bharti, Sanand Sasidharan

Electronic health records are written to support care, not retrieval. Across years of oncology notes, the primary diagnosis is restated in report after report, while the events that shape a patient's course, such as a recurrence or a change in therapy, may be recorded only once. A retriever that ranks passages by similarity to the query keeps returning the repeated material and can miss these transitions, that is, it exhibits temporal redundancy across historical narratives. We argue that longitudinal clinical retrieval should be treated as allocating a limited evidence budget across time, not just ranking by relevance. We separate the task into three objectives: temporal evidence allocation, candidate reconstruction, and clinical validation. In Study A (13 clinical queries over 20 patient records, with two-stage reconstruction), the Temporal-15 configuration reaches a milestone slot recall of 0.538, compared with 0.388 for the Global-50 baseline. However, it also retrieves far more evidence (496 versus 50 indexed units per query), so the gain cannot be credited to allocation alone. When evidence is held fixed, removing prompted precedence rules raises schema-order reversals from 0.010 to 0.080. A five-patient, single-query pilot with fixed budgets finds that wider temporal coverage does not reliably yield more dated milestones. Together, these results suggest that how much a record says about a period is a poor guide to how useful that period is as evidence. Because no expert reference timelines are available, our measurements describe retrieval behavior rather than clinical accuracy. This position and technical report presents the retrieval analysis behind the Onco-CONSTRUE framework and sets out a research agenda for budget-constrained longitudinal clinical retrieval.

摘要：

##### **Pairwise Learning Increases Data Efficiency in Deep Neural Networks**
2610.27669v1 by Robert Bain, Marjan Rashidi, Elizabeth Chrastil

Deep neural networks often fail at small-sample regression, collapsing to low-variance predictions that miss individual differences. Here, we introduce a pairwise comparison framework that reframes regression as aggregated relative rankings. This method improves data efficiency across model architectures and dataset sizes. On the UTKFace dataset (23k images), pairwise learning generates over one billion training comparisons without additional supervision. The paired ranking allows a lightweight ResNet10 trained from scratch on home hardware to achieve a 3.64-year MAE-surpassing previous models, including those with large-scale pretraining. For classification into categories, pairwise supervision consistently outperforms an otherwise identical 10-way crossentropy model on STL10 with 500-2000 labeled examples, improving accuracy by up to 5.2%. Overall, pairwise supervision enhances data efficiency and enables strong regression and classification performance when labeled data are limited, particularly in clinical settings.

摘要：

##### **Corpus Quality Improves, Retrieval Utility Does Not: An Ablation of Preprocessing and Refinement in a Speech-to-QA Pipeline**
2610.27652v1 by Wonsup Shin, Jingu Kim

Practitioners building training corpora with a pipeline of language models keep a preprocessing or refinement (post-process) stage when switching it on raises the corpus quality. We ablate a production pipeline that turns conference speech into question-answer pairs. The preprocessing stage rewrites the transcript, and the refinement stage filters the generated pairs. Switching each on and off gives four corpora from the same 40 Korean medical and finance sessions. On the corpus itself, quality rises as the stages switch on. Four LLM judges and six human domain experts rank the four corpora the same way, and the fully refined corpus leads by +0.18 and +0.22 on a 1-5 scale. We then ask whether that gain carries downstream. We supply each corpus to six models at inference and measure retrieval accuracy. The ranking does not hold. That stage also reads the whole session, so it almost doubles the cost of one. With both stages on, accuracy does not change. An equivalence test we declared in advance confirms this. The corpus itself is clearly valuable. It lifts retrieval accuracy from 0.231 to 0.748 in every model and domain we test. More than a third of its pairs carry knowledge that neither of two frontier models produces on its own. On our data, the null does not come from weak corpora. A rising score on a rubric of this kind did not predict what the corpus holds for a model.

Federated learning (FL) is a distributed machine learning paradigm that reduces transmission costs, mitigates cross-border data sharing restrictions, and enhances privacy by keeping raw data on local devices. Clients in FL systems train local models and share only updates, which are aggregated by a central server. While this setup limits direct data exposure, FL remains vulnerable to privacy attacks. Existing attack and defense strategies have largely been developed for centralized systems, and adapting them to FL introduces new challenges due to its unique architecture and training workflow. In this work, we first propose a model inversion attack on FL systems using Deep Convolutional Generative Adversarial Networks (DCGANs) in the context of large-scale medical imaging. Our method operates under realistic assumptions about the attacker's knowledge and capabilities. We then examine the effect of client-side differential privacy (DP) in mitigating privacy leakage and incorporate personalized FL to improve model performance under DP settings. Experiments on the Brain MRI ND-5 dataset show that DCGAN-based attacks can reconstruct semantically meaningful synthetic images, revealing significant risks to the privacy of training data. Applying DP reduces attack success but introduces an accuracy trade-off, which is further overcome by personalized FL at each client side. We also evaluate how different aggregation algorithms affect both attack vulnerability and defense effectiveness, underscoring the critical role of aggregation strategies. Starting from a small set of initial roots, MapSteal represents acquisition targets using structured states and recursively expands them through teacher-proposed directions. Question realization, proposal admission, and deduplication constrain the acquisition process, while root-aware scheduling organizes exploration under a fixed QA budget. The resulting question--answer pairs are used to adapt student models without requiring student feedback during acquisition. Experiments with Qwen3.5 students at 2B, 4B, and 9B scales show competitive downstream performance across six medical and cybersecurity benchmarks. On the 4B medical evaluation, Full MapSteal improves average accuracy by 1.17 percentage points over a simplified recursive acquisition variant. Cross-domain evaluations show that gains depend on the domain and evaluation panel. These results demonstrate the feasibility of structured, student-independent recursive acquisition while highlighting the distinction between generating acquisition targets and obtaining useful training supervision. Our code is available at \href{https://github.com/LabRAI/Mapsteal} {https://github.com/LabRAI/Mapsteal}.

##### **When Better Opportunities Backfire: Service Worker Responses to Parallel AI Deployment in Customer Acquisition**
2610.25603v1 by Xinyu Fu, Shulong Luo, Narayan Ramasubbu, Peng Shen, Xincheng Wang, Yucheng Wang

As AI voice agents get deployed in high-volume contact centers, firms can route low-intent leads to AI and allocate high-intent leads to human workers. Drawing on sequential search theory, we posit that AI-induced task allocation reshapes work, enriching opportunities facing human workers but also raising their reservation value for continuing with the lead in hand, making them less willing to persist. We study the effects of an AI voice-agent deployment at a Chinese online health-insurance brokerage using more than a half million customer outreach records surrounding the rollout. Adopting a regression-discontinuity-in-time design, we trace how worker behavior changes across the workday. Results show that the enrichment of human-facing opportunities concentrates early in the workday, raising the probability that a call is a high-intent lead by about 23 percentage points on the first three calls and fading to zero later. However, customer acquisition by human workers declines precisely where the enrichment is greatest, falling about 5 percentage points on the first three calls and flattening over the day. Consistent with the theorized reservation value mechanism, workers reallocate their earliest leads about 8 percentage points more often and persist less with them, shortening those calls by about 13%. A calibrated decomposition analysis further shows that the modest gain from improved lead ordering is outweighed by a much larger negative behavioral response. By reshaping the quality and within-workday distribution of opportunities, parallel AI deployment alters how workers pursue tasks, highlighting opportunity distribution as an important service operations state variable beyond workload and congestion.

##### **From Clinical Risk Prediction to Operational Decisions: An Uncertainty-Aware Framework for ICU Staffing**

Advances in electronic health records (EHR) and large-scale critical care databases have enabled the development of machine learning models for the early detection of patient deterioration. While these models often achieve strong predictive performance for outcomes such as sepsis and mortality, most studies evaluate them purely on discrimination metrics. This leaves a critical gap: how to systematically translate probabilistic clinical risk estimates into downstream operational decisions under real-world constraints, such as shift rigidity and hiring delays.
  This study proposes an uncertainty-aware anticipatory staffing framework that bridges short-term patient risk prediction and staff planning without altering the upstream predictive model. Instead of relying on naive deterministic aggregation or fixed safety buffers, the framework explicitly models dynamic predictive uncertainty and evaluates extreme shortage exposure using Conditional Value-at-Risk (CVaR). These aggregated, risk-calibrated demand signals are then embedded into an hourly rolling-horizon scheduling policy to generate delay-constrained staffing decisions.

Pareto frontier analysis illustrates how uncertainty-aware demand construction changes resource allocation under varying safety preferences, while stress testing confirms the framework's operational robustness even when subjected to systematic predictive bias in the form of calibration drift. ##### **From Prediction to Action: Population Health World Model for Digital Medicine**
2610.24662v1 by Xike Xie, Yu Li, Weizhong Yang, Amalie Dyda, Shaohua Kevin Zhou

Digital medicine now captures data that traces individual health trajectories and reveals population health dynamics through electronic health records, claims, remote monitoring, wearable devices, mobile devices, social sensing data, medical images, etc. But much of its modeling still centers on task-specific, outcome-specific, and prediction-specific estimation. The next frontier is not simply better prediction, but action in an evolving health world shaped by multiple decision-makers, heterogeneous populations, resource constraints, and feedback in care delivery. This is why digital medicine now needs world models: not only to estimate what will happen, but to represent how actions change what can happen next -- whom to prioritize, how to allocate scarce resources, how to adapt care across subgroups, and how interventions reshape future health trajectories and population dynamics.
  We call such a model population health world model (PHWM): a more foundational representation of an evolving health world in which health states, individual trajectories, population health dynamics, interventions, constraints, and feedback are intrinsically coupled. Building a PHWM endows three unmet structural gaps in current digital medicine: dynamic health-state modeling, population-level intervention intelligence, and validation, equity, and governance for action-oriented use. We argue that addressing these gaps requires three minimal capabilities: representation learning under action-dependent observation, multi-scale intervention intelligence, and validation with explicit decision boundaries, while highlighting the importance of social, contextual, and public-health sensing data for action-oriented digital medicine.

Drawing on lessons from social media, we argue that safety assessments must also address accumulative harms that develop through repeated use, recurring decisions, and feedback across interconnected deployments. We examine how related mechanisms may operate in AI systems, using conversational AI as a concrete setting where generation, semantic inference, and persistent memory may tighten feedback loops. Sycophancy, manipulation, and privacy loss provide a case study of how these risks may interact and accumulate. We therefore call for evaluation of cumulative patterns of use and deployment, individual and societal outcomes, careful causal inference, and proportionate safeguards that preserve useful assistance and informed user choice.

##### **The Self-Improvement Problem**
2610.21646v1 by Brian B. Moser, Stanislav Frolov, Tobias C. Nauen, Federico Raue, Andreas Dengel

This autonomy raises a fundamental question for safe self-improvement: can an automated gate like a reward model or an automated reviewer reliably decide whether a proposed update should be accepted? The difficulty is structural. The gate must be a terminating, computable procedure, while the property we ultimately care about, namely whether an update is a genuine semantic improvement, depends on potentially unbounded program behavior. We formalize this mismatch by modeling agents as programs and defining improvement in terms of a semantic score that need not be computable. We study two evaluation abstractions. The first is a certifier that approves or rejects an update. The second is a search procedure that, given access to a proposal generator, attempts to output some improving candidate if one exists. We show that, in the fully general setting, no terminating procedure can simultaneously guarantee reliability (never accepting a non-improvement) and non-conservatism (never missing an available improvement): self-referential constructions can force any such gate or searcher to fail. Randomized gates with bounded error face the same limit. We then identify a standard decidable regime used in practice (bounded evaluation and a finite proposal budget) under which exhaustive simulation yields a terminating search procedure with the desired guarantees. We also show the limit of this recovery: a chain of accepted updates can raise the bounded score in every round while the true score falls. Our results draw a boundary between unbounded, universal settings where correctness guarantees collapse and bounded settings where they can be recovered by design.

The Agent Status Protocol (ASP) defines a lightweight way for heterogeneous AI agents to report their operational state to a status server, and for observers such as dashboards to receive that state as soon as it changes. Agents push small JSON events over HTTP POST; the server exposes the aggregated state to observers over Server-Sent Events (SSE). Because an agent that has crashed, hung or been compromised cannot be trusted to report its own failure, ASP does not rely on self-reports alone: the server infers liveness from missed heartbeats, can detect agents that are alive but no longer making progress, and accepts observations from supervisors, guardrails, operators and peers, some of which take precedence over what the agent says about itself while others are shown alongside it. ASP models state along independent dimensions (lifecycle, health, task, floor, governance), so that an agent can be, for example, online, degraded, working on a task and waiting for the floor at the same time. It reuses existing vocabularies: task states from the Agent2Agent (A2A) protocol, floor states from the Open Floor Protocol (OFP), and attribute conventions from the OpenTelemetry GenAI semantic conventions. We investigate the design choices for amortization, including alternative generative-model constructions, reward estimation, hypothesis-space specification, multi-step objectives, and computational costs relative to training- and inference-time BED baselines. 2610.17951v1 by Ammar Bhilwarawala, Mainak Bandyopadhyay

Head circumference (HC) measured from 2D ultrasound is a routine marker of fetal growth, and automating it needs segmentations whose boundaries hold up on individual scans, not only on average. We present FETALFusion, a dual-path CNN-Mamba encoder-decoder for fetal head segmentation across acquisition types. It applies four-directional state-space scans only at the two highest-resolution stages, routes bottleneck features through domain-specific channel gates that are averaged at test time, and adds a weak landmark shape prior. We train jointly on transabdominal (HC-18) and transperineal (PSFHS) images and compare ten methods on a held-out test set of 471 images, each trained under one schedule and scored by surface distances at native resolution. FETALFusion reaches a Dice score of 0.963 and an HD95 of 2.10 mm without test-time augmentation or a domain label. It improves on U-Mamba on all five reported metrics (HD95 reduced by 0.31 mm; Holm-corrected ($p < 10^{-4}$), a lead that holds under test-time augmentation and across two independent training runs, and in the mean on U-Net, Attention U-Net and TransUNet. Residual encoders close the gap: a ResNet-34 UNet++ exceeds FETALFusion, and an ImageNet initialised ResNet-34 U-Net is best overall (HD95 1.75 mm). The ablation agrees. Calibrated against run-to-run variation, it attributes FETALFusion's measurable gains to the landmark shape prior and, on transperineal images, to the domain gates, while removing the state-space branch does not measurably change accuracy. HC from the predicted masks agrees with the clinical reference at an intraclass correlation of 0.9986.

摘要：

##### **AI-Enhanced Hyperspectral Imaging for Intraoperative Detection of Low-Grade Glioma: A Clinical Study**
2610.17944v1 by Siri Willems, Ishika Jain, Jens De Winne, Siri Luthman, Hamed Zivari Adab, Roeland Vandebriel, Anna Alperovich, Xiaohan Zhang, Ben Stoffelen, Tom De Schepper, Lien Solie, Steven De Vleeschouwer

Current image-guided neuronavigation techniques, such as intraoperative MRI and ultrasound, are limited by high costs, limited sensitivity and operational complexity. There is a clear need for more efficient systems to enhance intra-operative tumor detection and improve patient outcomes for intrinsic brain tumor resection. Whereas previous intraoperative brain HSI studies have predominantly investigated high-grade gliomas, glioblastomas, or mixed-grade cohorts, this clinical study focuses specifically on low-grade gliomas (LGGs). LGGs strongly resemble normal brain under white light conditions on the operative microscope. This study characterizes spectral differences between healthy and tumor tissue and provides a paired comparison of HSI- and RGB-based classification at matched tissue locations and under identical patient-level data splits. Tumor regions showed consistently lower reflectance, which may be associated with differences in hemoglobin concentration and oxygenation. A range of classification models was evaluated. The 3D CNN, which integrates spectral and spatial features, achieved the highest median ROC-AUC of 74.5%, compared with 67.9% for the spectral-only XGBoost model and 64.0% for the RGB-based model. These results support further investigation of HSI for low-grade glioma classification and quantify its performance relative to conventional RGB imaging in this cohort.

#### Abstracts
##### **Semantic Observability for Autonomous Systems Diagnostics**
2610.28611v1 by Jitendra Solanki, Anand Todkar, Ziran Min

Modern distributed microservice deployments generate massive volumes of heterogeneous, multi-format observability logs across physically and logically partitioned infrastructure. Isolating root causes during cascading failures remains an arduous task for Site Reliability Engineers (SREs), bottlenecked by manual keyword filtering and disjointed temporal correlation across monitoring dashboards. We introduce Semantic Observability, an autonomous multi-agent framework that unifies disparate log streams into a dynamic, queryable Property Graph Database. By modeling physical infrastructure topologies, logical software dependencies, and chronological event sequences as attributed nodes and directed relations, it bridges the semantic gap inherent in flat, unstructured log repositories. Building on this unified substrate, we deploy a stateful multi-agent system orchestrated through Directed Cyclic Graphs (DCGs). The system autonomously navigates the topology, applying causal graph algorithms and temporal back-traversal to isolate the precise blast radius of anomalies. The pruned subgraph is then interpreted by a Large Language Model (LLM) reasoning engine to generate contextualized, code-level Root Cause Analyses (RCA) and actionable remediations. This paper establishes the foundational system architecture, formal relational ontology, and multi-agent control loops of Semantic Observability. We show conceptually how the framework eliminates ambient telemetry noise, reduces Mean Time to Resolution (MTTR), compresses LLM token consumption, and prevents structural hallucinations. We also specify an open-source validation framework on standard benchmarks (LogHub and Train-Ticket), laying the groundwork for a forthcoming companion paper on exhaustive quantitative evaluation and production benchmarking.

Population-based evolution then selects which repositories to develop further by their performance on validation tasks the meta-agent never sees, favoring changes that generalize beyond the seen tasks. ##### **Learning Computational Structure for Sample-Efficient Prediction**

Statistical learning in high dimensions suffers from the curse of dimensionality. Fortunately, many functions of practical interest are compositionally sparse, meaning they admit a composition of local functions, each depending on only a few variables, allowing sample complexity to depend on local arity rather than ambient dimension. Yet realizing this benefit requires exploiting hidden compositional structure, which theory does not establish for practical gradient-based training. Across 32 additional targets generated by independently sampled computation graphs, LeWi is the most sample-efficient method not given the target graph, with 27% lower mean learning-curve area than dense networks. Dense networks perform better on targets with a single high-arity function. LeWi's representations and connectivity align with the target computation. On 17 world-model prediction tasks spanning control, navigation, and manipulation, LeWi beats the dense baseline on 16 and wins 99/119 baseline comparisons. These results show that jointly learning connectivity and weights can exploit computational structure to improve sample efficiency.

##### **Binary Discrete Diffusion: Candidate-Set Diffusion for Logic and Planning Tasks**

##### **Claims of emergence in AI should be grounded in information decomposition**
2610.28479v1 by Christoph Riedl, Fernando E. Rosas

When a language model develops capabilities not traceable to its individual components, when a multi-agent AI system outperforms each agent on its own, or when a human-AI pair succeeds where either fails in isolation, AI researchers describe it as a case of ``synergy'' or ``emergence''. However, these are usually informal assessments not supported by a theoretical framework, making these claims impossible to compare or even meaningfully dispute. Here we argue that \emph{information decomposition can serve as a unifying formal framework to operationalize assessments about synergy in AI systems}. Information decomposition provides a way to distinguish qualitatively different \emph{types} of information, including unique information available from individual components, redundant information shared across multiple components, and synergistic information accessible only from parts taken together. This gives claims of emergence a precise, falsifiable criterion: a system's behavior is emergent only if it carries synergistic information about task-relevant outcomes. We apply this lens to attention heads, autonomous agents, and human-AI teams, showing how it organizes existing knowledge across scales and substrates, while also generating cross-level conjectures that siloed fields would miss.

Across more than 35 compressed checkpoints from six open-weight LLMs-covering quantization and pruning-we find that compression redistributes ability profiles. Quantization consistently outperforms pruning in retention, but the practical meaning of that gap depends entirely on which ability an application requires. Based on our findings, deployers can choose compression methods by the ability profile their application needs, not just by a leaderboard average. We believe that ACE-Compression turns compression from a black-box accuracy drop into an interpretable intervention-one we can measure, understand, and ultimately improve.

Code translation converts source code from one programming language to another while preserving its functionality. These failures are especially harmful in translation because small semantic deviations can silently change program behavior. Guided by the taxonomy, we further propose HaluCodeTran, a mitigation framework that combines BM25-based retrieval of relevant translation examples with taxonomy-aware reflective refinement. The study provides an execution-tested characterization of code-translation hallucinations and a practical baseline for building more reliable LLM-based code migration tools.

##### **Factual Access States in Diffusion Language Models: From Mechanism to Knowledge Editing and Retrieval**
2610.27580v1 by Yuchen Guo, Zhuoyuan Mao, Yiwen Guo

We call it the factual access state. In autoregressive (AR) models the subject position enriches attributes largely independently of the relation, and relation-specific selection happens later, at the readout position. In masked diffusion language models (DLMs) we find the state is assembled differently, and much earlier: in the first attention layer the wording of the relation is routed into the position of whichever entity is visible and the early feed-forward layers look the fact up on this fused representation. KPHarness separates fast policy execution from a slow research loop that translates accumulated knowledge and probing evidence into bounded harness revisions. Each revision is compared with the incumbent under matched sandbox conditions and accepted only when the paired evaluation shows improvement. Recoveries supported by further validation are retained for cross-task reuse, while immediate interaction feedback and delayed episode-success credit update action- and knowledge-probing policies during adaptation. Experiments on Minecraft tasks spanning mining, combat, and crafting show improved task coverage over bare policy execution, a manually specified fixed harness, and knowledge-only harness evolution with non-adaptive probing. Evaluation on previously unseen tasks further shows improved task completion with the evolved harness, knowledge bank, and probing policies held fixed,

##### **More Memory, Worse Agents: Error Reproduction and Anti-Persistence in LLM Agents**
2610.27406v1 by Ruhan Wang, Kishan Panaganti, Dongruo Zhou

LLM agents can improve across tasks without updating model parameters by storing experience from their own executions in external memory and reusing it to guide later decisions. A central design question is whether to retain only observed trajectories and outcomes or also the rules, summaries, and skills inferred from them. Retaining these abstractions allows agents to reuse strategies without repeatedly deriving them from raw trajectories. However, errors in stored abstractions can propagate to subsequent decisions through retrieval and reuse. They can also shape execution trajectories, which then become the basis for new abstractions that reproduce the original errors. We term this feedback mechanism error reproduction: errors are regenerated through a cycle of abstraction, execution, and re-abstraction, extending their persistence beyond direct reuse of the original faulty knowledge. An adaptive selector uses prior outcomes to decide whether and how to synthesize guidance, which is discarded after execution. This prevents its direct reuse as a stored object, although indirect feedback through retained trajectories remains possible. Across different benchmarks and backbones, Anti-Persistence achieves higher overall held-out success and the lowest average LLM-call cost among the evaluated memory designs. We call the path one idea takes through this process, with the evidence and reasoning behind each step, its authentic research trajectory. Although it underlies every scientific contribution, it is rarely recorded. Existing LLM-based research agents generate an idea once, refine it against reviews, literature, or their own experimental results, or search over candidate ideas and experiments. In all of them the text of the idea evolves, while the evidence and reasoning that connected one version to the next are discarded. We propose the Research Progress Graph (RPG), a representation of authentic and complete research trajectories. An RPG records the evolution of an idea as typed research states connected by cognitive transitions, each annotated with a temporal index and the reasoning behind it, which makes this evolution an explicit object of study. We ground the representation in authentic trajectories whose steps their authors justify in annotations and papers. Comparing the representations of thirty-five research agents with the RPG, we find that each is an incomplete RPG; none keeps the state that changed, the evidence that changed it, and the reasoning connecting them as one evolving record. Modeling ideation with the RPG changes how research agents should be designed, evaluated, and trained. Agents predict the next research state instead of a complete idea, ideas are evaluated as trajectories instead of final texts, and authentic trajectories serve as training data. ##### **How Benchmark Graph Construction Shapes Multi-Hop KG Navigation: A Controlled Audit of PathQuestion and PathQuestion-Large**
2610.26399v1 by Eduin E. Hernandez, Sergio A. Diaz, Luis F. Garcia, Nurassyl Askar, Stefano Rini

Multi-hop knowledge-graph question answering (KGQA) benchmarks are often treated as fixed language-and-reasoning tasks, but for navigation-based methods, difficulty also depends on the graph in which those tasks are instantiated. This makes graph construction a consequential but often entangled part of the navigation task. To isolate this effect, we audit PathQuestion (PQ) and PathQuestion-Large (PQL) as graph-navigation benchmarks under a controlled intervention: we hold questions, topic entities, released answers, released path annotations, and family-level splits fixed while replacing each released task-filtered graph with the larger source KG distributed with the benchmark. ##### **Selective Transfer through Sequence Distillation: From Benchmark Knowledge to Backdoor Behaviour**
2610.26311v1 by Jonibek Mansurov, Sholpan Bolatzhanova, Filbert Aurelian Tjiaranta, Vallerie Alexandara Putra, Alham Fikri Aji

We study whether different hidden teacher properties survive the same distillation channels. Specifically, we compare two properties: benchmark-associated knowledge and refusal backdoors, and show that distillation treats them very differently. Teachers fine-tuned on benchmark data successfully pass that knowledge to students through all four distillation channels we test, including when students are trained only on teacher-generated number sequences containing no natural language or domain content whatsoever. Refusal backdoors tell the opposite story: backdoored teachers achieve near-perfect triggered refusal, yet sequence-distilled students inherit essentially none of this behaviour. ##### **CoRS: Fast Neural Flexible Job-Shop Scheduling via Composite Interval State Reuse**
2610.26239v1 by Jiang Sutong, Zhao Peng, Wu Xuan, Wang Yan, Wang Jiaqi, Zhang Rui, Jiang Yuan, Zhou You, Wang Di

We present CoRS, which decouples them: an interval-level graph representation is reused across multiple sequential decisions, while a stepwise feasibility state is updated after every action. By providing more comprehensive structural information of the scheduling problem, these features improve the model's perception of overall problem characteristics. A lightweight OrderMLP integrated into the GPT backbone learns to predict these priorities from attention, incorporating order learning into the architecture within a single training run. Training gradually shifts toward the learned order, which is then frozen for subsequent autoregressive training and generation. The same mechanism adapts across modalities: adaptively it recovers near-L2R dependency structure in language, learns anatomically organized factorizations on human pose, and discovers a factorization on tabular data that places statistically and semantically related fields nearby, while improving downstream modeling metrics across all domains. The code is available at https://github.com/Chenhe-yang/OrderGPT/.

摘要：

##### **TESS: LLM-guided Translation, Exploration, Simulation, and Search for Embodied Task Planning**
2610.25930v1 by Peiran Xu, Yixiang Yang, Jinghan Li, Fei Li, Cao Sheng, JiaZhong Yu, Yadong Mu

In this work we focus on the problem of embodied task planning, which requires an agent to accomplish a complex task by separating it into atomic actions. Recent studies have developed LLM-based methods to address this problem. However, the executability and correctness of the generated plans remain suboptimal in complex environments and tasks. On the other hand, classical search algorithms have the potential to solve the planning problems perfectly, yet they require fully observable environments, known state transitions and goal state, which are often unattainable in general task domains. Therefore, we propose to implement an LLM-guided search algorithm that makes the search process possible and efficient by incorporating LLMs' commonsense knowledge. Our method leverages LLMs to (1) translate natural language instructions into formatted goal conditions, (2) facilitate efficient exploration in partially observable environments, (3) implement a rule-based world simulator, and (4) prune the space of candidate actions to improve search efficiency. Experimental results on VirtualHome and ALFWorld demonstrate its superior performance.

摘要：

##### **FisherEdit: Fisher Information Guided Knowledge Editing for Large Language Models**
2610.25914v1 by Yizhou Yang, Jiahong Fu, Zhiwu Wang, Kailong Fan, Haokun Lin, Yichen Wu, Zhenan Sun, Renzhen Wang, Deyu Meng

Sequential knowledge editing in Large Language Models (LLMs) often leads to a degradation of general capabilities due to cumulative parameter interference. In this paper, we revisit prominent projection-based methods like AlphaEdit and reveal a surprising mismatch: their robustness is primarily driven by update regularization rather than the intended null-space projection. Based on this insight, we propose FisherEdit, a theoretically grounded method built on Fisher geometry, which replaces coarse activation-space projections with importance-aware regularization in parameter space. It penalizes changes to sensitive parameters more strongly while retaining flexibility for new facts, and admits an efficient row-wise closed-form solution. Experiments on Llama-3, Qwen2.5, and Olmo-3 across multiple benchmarks demonstrate that FisherEdit achieves state-of-the-art performance while better preserving model stability and downstream task accuracy.

摘要：

##### **When Should AI Chime In? Benchmarking Timely Contribution of Proactive Agents in Multi-Party Chat**
2610.25896v1 by Ruotong Wang, Brian S. Yu, Xinyi Zhou, Joseph Chee Chang, Pao Siangliulue, Jonathan Bragg, Liwei Jiang, Amy X Zhang

Proactive agents can support group collaboration by surfacing useful information without waiting to be asked, but even relevant contributions can be disruptive if poorly timed. In this work, we isolate the timing question: given a fixed candidate contribution such as a clarification or relevant fact, when should an AI agent chime in? We introduce PITCH (Proactive Intervention Timing in CHat), to our knowledge the first benchmark with large-scale human judgments of proactive agent timing collected from participants in the same unscripted multi-party conversations they took part in. To collect high-quality judgments, we design a novel interface in which 2-4 people chat synchronously, author candidate AI contribution messages, place their own and others' contributions into the chat history, and then deliberate as a group about the best placement for each message. PITCH contains 384 candidate contributions and 1,091 judgments, each marking an ideal turn, an acceptable window, or that a contribution should not appear, from 89 participants engaged in 35 conversations. Evaluating 15 models across 28 configurations, we find that while models can place contributions well when given the entire conversation history, they systematically chime in too early when asked to decide live as the conversation unfolds: best-turn accuracy falls from 57.8% to 19.0%, with nearly half of placements occurring before every participant's acceptable window. In comparison, lay humans deciding live are more accurate (23.7% vs 19.0%), and fewer of their placements are premature (39.7% vs 46.4%). Model reasoning traces show that 93% of intervention justifications cite relevance, while almost none consider waiting. By providing a human-grounded benchmark, our work reveals gaps in agents' ability to integrate into group settings and points towards proactive agents that can reason explicitly about appropriate timing.

摘要：

##### **Are Self-Evolving Agents Continual Learners?**
2610.25858v1 by Xiaoyin Chen, Yizheng Huang, Yinglun Xu, Ziyun Zhao, Emmett McQuinn, Julie Liu, Hope Yao

Large language model agents are often deployed in environments where new knowledge, tools, and workflows keep arriving. Such an agent is therefore expected to adapt continually, learning new tasks as they arrive without forgetting earlier ones. This resembles the continual learning problem, but at a pace that conventional gradient updates cannot sustain. Nonparametric self-evolving agents are a natural alternative: they adapt by rewriting their own harness, such as prompts, memory, and skills, from task feedback while the model weights stay frozen. We study whether such agents are effective continual learners across four drifting task streams that shift in topic, workflow, or domain (built from BIRD, BFCL, and ScienceWorld). Evaluating three representative methods spanning experience retrieval and self-modification (ReasoningBank, GEPA, and Meta-Harness), we find that although self-evolution improves aggregate performance on most streams, higher plasticity consistently comes at the cost of stability. Specifically, self-modifying methods apply global edits to a shared prompt or workspace, interfering with unseen tasks and overwriting earlier knowledge as the stream shifts. Conversely, similarity-based memory retrieval localizes updates across distant domains, but still retrieves foreign memories on unseen tasks and suffers from retrieval collision when similar queries require conflicting workflows. These findings show that current self-evolving agents remain incomplete continual learners.

摘要：

##### **A Cue's Hidden Price: Auditing Seller Influence on AI Shoppers**
2610.25843v1 by Felix Buecheler, Gerardo Berbeglia, Mingyu Guo

Large language models are increasingly asked to shop on a user's behalf, which lets seller-controlled content such as badges, endorsements, and framing steer what they recommend. That poses the question at the core of generative engine optimization (GEO): not whether seller content shifts an agent's choice, but how much. The usual gauge of that influence, how often a cue wins the choice, has a blind spot: once a cue is strong enough to be picked almost every time, the choice rate can no longer show how much stronger one cue is than another. We introduce price-equivalent (PE) auditing, which removes this ceiling by pricing the cue in money. In a counterbalanced, position-controlled forced choice, we raise the cued product's price until it is chosen only half the time, and report that premium as the cue's price-equivalent: a formally defined, policy-conditioned parity threshold that makes very different cues comparable. Priced this way, cues that look equally strong at equal price differ by a wide margin, so choice-rate audits substantially under-resolve seller influence. We run the audit over 113,472 trials, seven models across families, and two product categories. To our knowledge, we also run the first price-equivalent audit of a decision-native model, Jev, which outputs a choice probability directly instead of generating text; strikingly, its profile is unlike any text model we tested, resisting most cues yet remaining susceptible to the strongest social-proof claims. We release the stimuli, logs, and code as a benchmark that reports each model's cue-price profile rather than a single score, giving developers and auditors a common, reproducible way to measure how content moves AI shopping agents.

摘要：

##### **Where RL Breaks: Localizing and Repairing Failures via Targeted Continued Pretraining**
2610.25836v1 by Xinyi Wang, Danqi Chen

Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) is widely used to improve reasoning in language models, yet growing evidence suggests that it mostly sharpens what the base model can already do. This raises a practical question: when RL fails, what is the base model missing? We look inside the RL policy's own rollouts and find that failures are localized: whether a rollout fails is strongly predicted by a small set of characteristic n-grams, ranging from reflection phrases such as "let's recheck" to specific operations such as "the inverse of A". These failures persist throughout RL training, although they are not beyond the model's reach: other sampled answers to the same question are often correct. Building on this observation, we use the failure $n$-grams to retrieve text from the pretraining corpus and continue pretraining the base model on it before RL. The resulting corpus has only 35M tokens, 3--4 orders of magnitude smaller than typical mid-training. We evaluate this approach on OLMo2 and Qwen3 models trained with RL on (1) GSM8K; (2) a mixture of math, commonsense, and knowledge questions; and (3) general math and science reasoning. Despite its size, the corpus yields consistent gains: our approach outperforms continued pretraining on the same amount of text sampled from the pretraining corpus across 10 out of 11 settings, including by $2.0$ points on GSM8K when inserted before mid-training. The retrieved text also transfers across model families: text retrieved for OLMo2-1B's failures improves Qwen3-4B on the mixture by $1.9$ points over the same baseline. Together, our results suggest that where RL fails is a cheap and effective signal for selecting pretraining data.

摘要：

##### **Modal Sense Disambiguation in Language Models: The Role of Context, Training Domain, and Input Frequency**
2610.25816v1 by Ezgi Başar, Yevgen Matusevych, Annemarie van Dooren, Arianna Bisazza

Modals such as may and must can take on different senses depending on discourse context, alternating between root interpretations (e.g., permission, obligation) and epistemic interpretations (e.g., knowledge, belief). While modals are known to be notoriously difficult for children to acquire, limited research exists on whether language models (LMs) can reliably distinguish between different modal senses. We evaluate seven LMs that vary in architecture, size, and training data type using vector-based similarity and surprisal measures across four datasets. Specifically, we investigate whether LMs can leverage contextual information to disambiguate modal senses and whether their performance reflects the distribution of modal senses in their training data. We show that larger LMs trained on diverse text successfully leverage additional contextual information when available. In the absence of context, they tend to favor the dominant sense based on corpus statistics. Smaller developmentally plausible LMs, including those trained on child-directed language (CDL), fail to show consistent sensitivity to modal sense distinctions and do not benefit from context to the same degree.

摘要：

##### **SAR: Learning Global Item-Space Matching and Geometry-Aware Placement Ranking for Buffered Online 3D Bin Packing**
2610.25795v1 by Xinpeng Lei, Jin Zhang, Yizhe Yang, Yihong Duan, Fucheng Wan

Online 3D bin packing with a bounded buffer requires irreversible item-selection and placement decisions under limited knowledge of future arrivals. Feasibility alone is insufficient, as feasible actions can produce different residual-space structures and subsequent packing opportunities. A central architectural question is how to allocate spatial reasoning between item selection and placement evaluation. We propose Spatial Affordance Retrieval (SAR), a novel learning-based framework that coordinates global item-space matching with geometry-aware placement ranking. A voxel-token spatial encoder represents 3D container occupancy, enabling a picker to match buffered items to the residual space without requiring item-specific placement proposals as inputs. After item selection, a loader ranks feasible rotation-position candidates using candidate geometry, local geometric features (LGF), and inter-candidate comparison, without directly accessing global spatial tokens. The feasible candidate set connects the two stages, which are jointly optimized under a shared long-horizon packing return. On the Random, JD, and SF datasets, the best SAR variants improve utilization over the strongest evaluated online baselines by 0.96-4.20 percentage points under the no-buffer setting (K=1). With a buffer (K=5), the best utilization rates across SAR variants reach 93.55%, 91.04%, and 90.41%, respectively. Ablations support the complementary roles of global item-space matching and geometry-aware placement evaluation, counterfactual analysis suggests that learned item selection favors greater future packing potential, and experiments demonstrate compatibility with multiple candidate generators.

摘要：

##### **Does Unlearning Survive Collaboration? Robust Unlearning for Shared-Model LLM Teams**
2610.25731v1 by Hengsong Liu, Liangyu Teng, Jingnan Cai, Yuntian Shi, Jingyu Zhang, Yang Liu, Jing Liu, Liang Song

LLM unlearning is essential for removing sensitive or unwanted knowledge while preserving useful capabilities. As LLM teams are increasingly used to solve complex problems, forgetting must remain effective during collaboration. Despite advances in forgetting effectiveness, efficiency, and robustness to relearning, team deployment remains underexplored. We find that collaboration weakens forgetting: final-answer evaluations miss recoverable evidence in team messages, including complementary clues supporting joint recovery. We establish theoretically and confirm empirically that controlling final answers or individual messages does not ensure low recovery from their combination. To address this challenge, we propose Aggregation-Conditioned Unlearning (ACU). ACU trains a shared model using reconstruction feedback from individual and pooled messages, prioritizes views with higher recovery risk, and uses replacement-based credit assignment while protecting direct and collaborative utility. TOFU evaluates trajectory-level recovery, while WMDP-bio tests native forgetting and retained utility. On TOFU, ACU lowers full-trajectory recovery from 41.8% to 19.3% in ROUGE-L recall and factual recovery from 34.2% to 18.7% versus output-supervised training, with comparable retained-task performance. These results demonstrate that ACU curbs information leakage in LLM teams and generalizes across team sizes and readers.

摘要：

##### **MapSteal: Map-based Autonomous Knowledge Stealing Attack Against Large Language Models**
2610.25626v1 by Zhengao Li, Chuxu Zhang, Yushun Dong

Knowledge acquisition from black-box large language models (LLMs) offers a practical approach to adapting smaller student models. However, many existing methods rely on predefined queries or student feedback to guide acquisition, leaving autonomous expansion of knowledge targets an important challenge. We propose \textbf{MapSteal}, a student-independent framework for structured recursive knowledge acquisition. Starting from a small set of initial roots, MapSteal represents acquisition targets using structured states and recursively expands them through teacher-proposed directions. Question realization, proposal admission, and deduplication constrain the acquisition process, while root-aware scheduling organizes exploration under a fixed QA budget. The resulting question--answer pairs are used to adapt student models without requiring student feedback during acquisition. Experiments with Qwen3.5 students at 2B, 4B, and 9B scales show competitive downstream performance across six medical and cybersecurity benchmarks. On the 4B medical evaluation, Full MapSteal improves average accuracy by 1.17 percentage points over a simplified recursive acquisition variant. Cross-domain evaluations show that gains depend on the domain and evaluation panel. These results demonstrate the feasibility of structured, student-independent recursive acquisition while highlighting the distinction between generating acquisition targets and obtaining useful training supervision. Our code is available at \href{https://github.com/LabRAI/Mapsteal} {https://github.com/LabRAI/Mapsteal}.

摘要：

##### **Assertion and Belief: Moore's Paradox in Language Models**
2610.25586v1 by Anshun Asher Zheng, Weili Cao, Fangcong Yin, David Beaver, Junyi Jessy Li

Moore's paradox \citep{moore1942} concerns sentences such as ``It is raining, but I don't believe that it is,'' which may well be true yet cannot be sensibly asserted. The absurdity lies not in the content but in the act, since to assert is to vouch for one's own belief. This makes the paradox a test of whether language models take the speech act into account when computing meaning. We present the first test of Moore's paradox in LLMs, evaluating 12 models on a curated set of Moorean sentences in varied contexts. We find that models largely recognize the infelicity of Moorean sentences, and that the judgement is not tied to a memorized form and tracks the speech act. Using the Rational Speech Act framework (RSA; \citealt{goodmanfrank2016}), we then test whether these judgements arise from a listener reasoning about a speaker. We find the models' own listeners do not reason about their own speakers, and their reasoning traces suggest that they instead recall knowledge about the paradox and apply rules therein. The models thus appear to have learned the paradox as a fact about sentences, not as a requirement on speakers.
